and
F_{4,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
, and it means that
C_{10,7}
and
F_{7,1}
are equivalent Lorentz tensors.
As always, we really have an infinite number of equivalences between zero-forms
C_{10,7,1,\dots,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{9,6})
and
F_{7,1,1,\dots,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{6})
but for our purposes it will suffice to consider only (
5.14
).

On-shell, the (Lorentz) irreducibility properties of
F_{7,1}
are exchanged under (
5.14
) with the analogous constraints on
C_{10,7}
as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{Tr}^{7}(C_{10,7})=0\;\;\\
C_{10,7}\text{ is $\text{GL}(11)$ irreducible}\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;F_{7,1}\text{ is $\text{GL}(11)$ irreducible}\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}(F_{7,1})=0\end{cases}

(5.15)

This is essentially the same as (
4.23
).
As a result, the equation of motion for
A_{9,6}
is equivalent to the higher trace constraint

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}^{7}(C_{10,7})=C_{a_{1}\dots a_{4}b_{1}\dots b_{7},}{}^{b_{1}\dots b_{7}}=\partial^{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A_{a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}b_{1}\dots b_{7}],}{}^{b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}=0\;.

(5.16)

Duality relation between
A_{9,3}
and
A_{9,6}
.

Using the zero-form relations (
3.5
), (
4.22
), and (
5.14
), we find that the primary zero-forms
C_{10,4}
and
C_{10,7}
are related by

\displaystyle C_{a[10],b[7]}\>\propto\>\varepsilon_{b[7]}{}^{c[4]}C_{a[10],c[4]}\;.

(5.17)

Their on-shell properties are exchanged under (
5.17
) as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{Tr}^{4}(C^{10,4})=0\;\;\\
C^{10,4}\text{ is $\text{GL}(11)$ irreducible}\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;C^{10,7}\text{ is $\text{GL}(11)$ irreducible}\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}^{7}(C^{10,7})=0\end{cases}

(5.18)

We can combine the three first-order duality relations equations (
3.5
), (
4.97
) and (
5.10
) into a single relation between
A_{9,3}
and
A_{9,6}
that takes the form

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{c_{1}\dots c_{10}}{}_{[a_{1}}\omega_{a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}]|c_{1}\dots c_{10}}\propto\omega^{b_{1}\dots b_{6}|}{}_{a_{1}\dots a_{4}b_{1}\dots b_{6}}\;.

(5.19)

It is useful to write this relation in the form

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}_{[a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}|a_{4}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{4}}{}^{b_{1}\dots b_{7}}\,\widetilde{\omega}_{[b_{1}\dots b_{6}|b_{7}]}\;,

(5.20)

where
\widetilde{\omega}_{[3]}{}^{1}
and
\widetilde{\omega}_{[6]}{}^{1}
are defined in terms of
\omega_{[3]}{}^{10}
and
\omega_{[6]}{}^{10}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}|b}:=\varepsilon_{b}{}^{c_{1}\dots c_{10}}\,\omega_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}|c_{1}\dots c_{10}}\;,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}_{a_{1}\dots a_{6}|b}:=\varepsilon_{b}{}^{c_{1}\dots c_{10}}\,\omega_{a_{1}\dots a_{6}|c_{1}\dots c_{10}}\;.

(5.21)

Equations (
3.5
), (
4.97
), (
5.10
) and (
5.20
) populate the following array of duality relations:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\vskip 2.84526ptF_{4}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega_{[3]}{}^{10}\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow\\
F_{7}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega_{[6]}{}^{10}\end{matrix}

(5.22)

This will be extended infinitely in Section
5.4
where first-order on-shell duality relations for all higher dual fields in the three-form and six-form sectors will be worked out.

5.2
Unfolding the field
h_{9,8,1}
at level six

There are nine fields in the
E_{11}
non-linear realisation at level six:
h_{9,8,1}
,
B_{10,6,2}
,
B_{10,7,1}
,
B_{10,8}
,
C_{11,4,3}
,
C_{11,5,1,1}
, two copies of
C_{11,6,1}
, and
C_{11,7}
.
Here we will obtain the unfolded formulation of the higher dual gravity field
h_{9,8,1}
.
In order to do so, we introduce a set of variables

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{a_{1}\dots a_{8},b}\;,\;\omega_{[8]}{}^{a_{1}\ldots a_{10},b}\;,\;X_{[1]}{}^{a_{1}\ldots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{9}}\;,\;C^{a_{1}\ldots a_{10},b_{1}\ldots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2}}\;,\;...

(5.23)

where the primary zero-form
C_{10,9,2}
is the first zero-form in the module

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}(h_{9,8,1})=\big\{C^{(n)}_{10,9,2,1^{n}}\,\big|\;n\in\mathbb{N}\,\big\}=\big\{C^{(0)}_{10,9,2},C^{(1)}_{10,9,2,1},C^{(2)}_{10,9,2,1,1},\dots\big\}\;.

(5.24)

The first three unfolded equations are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{a[8],b}+h_{c[2]}\,\omega_{[8]}{}^{c[2]\langle a[8],b\rangle}=0\;,

(5.25a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b}+h_{c[8]}\,X_{[1]}{}^{a[10],c[8]b}=0\;,

(5.25b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[9]}+h_{c[2]}\,C^{a[10],b[9],c[2]}=0\;.

(5.25c)

In (
5.25a
) the angled brackets denote the projection of the final nine indices of
\omega_{[8]}{}^{10,1}
onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[8,1]
tableau.
It may be clearer to rewrite (
5.25a
) as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{a_{1}\dots a_{8},b}+h_{c_{1}c_{2}}\left(\omega_{[8]}{}^{c_{1}c_{2}a_{1}\dots a_{8},b}-\omega_{[8]}{}^{c_{1}c_{2}[a_{1}\dots a_{8},b]}\right)=0\;.

(5.26)

The gauge transformations of the above equations are given by

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{a[8],b}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{a[8],b}-h_{c[2]}\,\alpha_{[7]}{}^{c[2]\langle a[8],b\rangle}\;,

(5.27a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[7]}{}^{a[10],b}-h_{c[8]}\,\beta^{a[10],c[8]b}\;,

(5.27b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[9]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta^{a[10],b[9]}\;.

(5.27c)

Schematically, we once again decompose everything in terms of irreducible components:

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{8,1}

\displaystyle=h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}+\widehat{A}_{10,7,1}+\widehat{A}_{10,8}+\widehat{A}_{11,6,1}+\widehat{A}_{11,7}\;,

(5.28a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{[8]}{}^{8,1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(1)}_{8,8,1}+\lambda^{(2)}_{9,7,1}+\lambda^{(3)}_{9,8}+\lambda^{(4)}_{10,6,1}+\lambda^{(5)}_{10,7}+\lambda^{(6)}_{11,5,1}+\lambda^{(7)}_{11,6}\;,

(5.28b)

\displaystyle\alpha_{[7]}{}^{10,1}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{(1)}_{10,7,1}+\alpha^{(2)}_{10,8}+\alpha^{(3)}_{11,6,1}+\alpha^{(4)}_{11,7}\;.

(5.28c)

The variable
e_{[9]}{}^{8,1}
contains the irreducible field
h_{9,8,1}
alongside four extra fields:
\widehat{A}_{10,7,1}
,
\widehat{A}_{10,8}
,
\widehat{A}_{11,6,1}
, and
\widehat{A}_{11,7}
.
These extra fields can be set to zero using the components of
\alpha_{[7]}{}^{10,1}
so that only
h_{9,8,1}
remains, whereafter there will remain some residual gauge symmetry and the
\alpha
parameters will be related to first derivatives of the
\lambda
parameters.

It is useful to denote the number of higher dualisations with a superscript, distinguishing the first higher dual graviton
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
at level six from the dual graviton
h_{8,1}
at level three.
Here we propose a first-order on-shell duality relation between the fields
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
and
h_{8,1}
in terms of their first-order connections:

\displaystyle\omega^{(1)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{8}|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c}\propto\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d}\,\omega_{c|a_{1}\dots a_{8}d}\;.

(5.29)

As for all the duality relations that we propose in this paper, we are working at the linearised level so the constant of proportionality can be absorbed by a redefinition of the fields.
However, the tensor structure and the precise coefficients in the full non-linear relations would be fixed by
E_{11}
symmetry, as explained in the introduction.

In the same way that (
4.97
) and (
5.10
) hold exactly when the parameters are constrained to be pure gauge-for-gauge, our higher duality relation (
5.29
) holds exactly in a gauge where
\beta^{10,9}
is related to
\alpha_{[7]}{}^{10,1}
and
\alpha^{9}
via the constraint
12
12

12

As in previous sections, we rescale
p
-forms by factors
p!
when writing equations in components.

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{8}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c}-\beta_{b_{1}\dots b_{10},a_{1}\dots a_{8}c}\propto\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{c}\alpha_{a_{1}\dots a_{8}d}\;,

(5.30)

where the constant of proportionality is the same as that of (
5.29
).
This constraint is invariant under gauge-for-gauge transformations.
As at previous levels, the constraints on the gauge parameters lead to extra freedom at the level of the fields, hence the extra fields in (
5.29
).

Taking derivatives of (
5.29
) leads to a gauge-invariant relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{d}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}h_{b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}\;.

(5.31)

The equation of motion for
h_{8,1}
was given in (
3.3
) and under (
5.31
) it is equivalent to

\displaystyle\partial^{[c}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{8}d],}{}^{d]}=0\;.

(5.32)

Moreover, going back to (
5.31
), antisymmetring
d
with
b[9]
or
c[2]
causes the right-hand side to vanish, leading to two on-shell constraints for the
h_{9,8,1}
field:

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial^{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{9}],}{}^{b_{2}\dots b_{9}],}{}_{c_{2}]}=0\;,

\displaystyle\partial^{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{8}c_{1}c_{2}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],}{}^{c_{2}]}=0\;.

(5.33)

Together, (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) are the equations of motion for the higher dual graviton
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
whose Young tableau contains more than two columns.
As such, we have more than one equation of motion for the
h_{9,8,1}
field, each with three derivatives, and they are independent of each other.

Solving equations (
5.25a
), (
5.25b
) and (
5.25c
) for the primary zero-form
C_{10,9,2}\in\mathscr{T}(h_{9,8,1})
in terms of
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
and the extra fields, we find that it is given by the curvature tensor

\displaystyle C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2}}=\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}

(5.34)

up to a factor.
Since we are unfolding on-shell,
C_{10,9,2}
obeys higher trace constraints that will be equivalent to the
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
equations of motion.
Moreover,
C_{9,2}
and its descendents
C_{9,2,1,\dots,1}
are irreducible Lorentz representations, so they are traceless and satisfy over-antisymmetrisation constraints for GL
(11)
irreducible tensors.
It is useful to rewrite (
5.31
) as a relation between
C_{10,9,2}\in\mathscr{T}(h_{9,8,1})
and
C_{9,2,1}\in\mathscr{T}(h_{8,1})
where we recall that
C_{9,2,1}
is really a projection of the gradient of the primary zero-form
C_{9,2}
:

\displaystyle C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\,C_{b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2},d}\;.

(5.35)

Under this relation, the on-shell properties of the zero-forms are exchanged as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{2,3}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{2}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{9}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,2}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,3}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2}(C_{9,2,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{1,2}(C_{9,2,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{2,3}(C_{9,2,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{1,3}(C_{9,2,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3}(C_{9,2,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3}(C_{9,2,1})=0\end{cases}

(5.36)

We use
\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j}
to denote a trace on columns
i
and
j
, and
\sigma_{i,j}
denotes over-antisymmetrisation of column
i
with one index in column
j>i
.
For example, one can write
\sigma_{1,3}(h_{9,8,1})
in place of
h_{[a_{1}\dots a_{9}|,b_{1}\dots b_{8},|c]}
.
A mixed-symmetry field
\phi
is GL
(11)
irreducible if and only if
\sigma_{i,j}(\phi)=0
for all
i
and
j
with
i<j
.
Thus the higher trace constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{9}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{2}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3}(C_{10,9,2})=0\;,

(5.37)

are equivalent to the linearised equations of motion (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) of the higher dual field
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
when the primary zero-form
C_{10,9,2}
is expressed as the curvature tensor (
5.34
).

Before moving on, we will clarify the linearised equations of motion (
5.37
).
It may seem strange that we have three independent third-order equations rather than just one second-order equation, but this is unsurprising from the perspective of reference
[
90
]
.
The idea is that the higher dual field
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
propagates the correct degrees of freedom when its curvature obeys all three equations.
Two of the equations arise from the Bianchi identities for the dual graviton
h_{8,1}
and the third equation appears when taking a gradient of the dual gravity equation (
3.3
).
Note that
B_{10,1,1}
at level four also has three blocks of indices and hence more than one equation of motion, i.e. the complete tracelessness of its curvature
C_{11,2,2}
in (
4.2
).

Note that a Lagrangian formulation would be different.
Higher dual action principles require extra fields in addition to the irreducible higher dual field alone, so instead of one equation of motion for the higher dual field, there are several.
An action in four dimensions for the higher dual graviton that is second-order in derivatives was found in
[
74
]
.
We found that an extra field is present inside this action, and neither of the two could be eliminated.
There were two standard second-order equations of motion, one for each of the two field.

Working backwards, we can integrate the
b[9]
column of (
5.31
) and use the Poincaré lemma again on
c[2]
to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{8},|c_{2}]}+\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},|c_{2}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{d}\partial_{[c_{1}|}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{8},|c_{2}]}\;,

(5.38)

up to some arbitrary
\Xi_{7|10,1}
tensor field.
Imposing the shift

\displaystyle\Xi_{b_{1}\dots b_{7}|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c}\;\longmapsto\;\Xi_{b_{1}\dots b_{7}|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c}-8\,\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{7}d,c}\;,

(5.39)

allows us to write equation (
5.38
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{8},|c_{2}]}+\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},|c_{2}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(9\,\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[d}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}],c_{2}]}\right)\;.

(5.40)

The
c[2]
column can now be integrated, leading to a first-order relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{8},c}+\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(9\,\partial_{[d}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}],c}+\partial_{c}\Theta_{db_{1}\dots b_{8}}\right)\;.

(5.41)

Comparing this with the previous duality relation in the gravity sector (
3.76
), we identify the terms in the parentheses with the connection
\omega_{[1]}{}^{8}
in (
3.21a
).
Moreover,
\Theta_{9}
is identified with the extra field
\widehat{A}_{9}
in (
3.25
) and the irreducible components of
\Xi_{7|10,1}
are identified with the extra fields in (
5.28a
).
Solving (
5.25a
) for
\omega_{[8]}{}^{10,1}=\omega^{(1)}{}_{[8]}{}^{10,1}
(again using a superscript to denote the number of higher dualisations) leads to

\displaystyle\omega^{(1)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{8}|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c}=\partial_{[b_{1}}h^{(1)}_{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],a_{1}\dots a_{8},c}+\partial_{[a_{1}}\Xi_{a_{2}\dots a_{8}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c}

(5.42)

on the left-hand side of (
5.41
) up to certain factors, while the quantity on the right-hand side in parentheses is the solution (
3.3
) of equation (
3.21a
).
Thus we have integrated up from the equations of motion to obtain the duality relations.

Looking back at equation (
5.40
), one can antisymmetrise
a[10]
with
c_{1}
to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{2}\dots a_{11}],c}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{11}}\eta^{c_{1}d}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[d}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}],c_{2}]}\;,

(5.43)

which vanishes on-shell due to the dual gravity equation of motion (
3.3
).
This implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c}=\partial_{[a_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{2}\dots a_{10}],c}\;,

(5.44)

for some tensor
\xi_{7|9,1}
whose irreducible components have the same Young tableaux as all but one of the irreducible differential gauge parameters in (
5.28b
) and (
5.27a
).
Importantly, these are the only parameters in the gauge transformations of the extra fields (
5.28a
), and (
5.41
) can now be written in the form

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(1)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{8},c}+\partial_{[a_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{8}]|a_{2}\dots a_{10}],c}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(9\,\partial_{[d}h_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}],c}+\partial_{c}\Theta_{db_{1}\dots b_{8}}\right)\;.

(5.45)

We propose that the gravity sector of the non-linear realisation of
E_{11}
should be extended to include the on-shell duality relations (
3.76
) and (
5.29
) that are summarised as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\omega_{[1]}{}^{2}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega_{[1]}{}^{9}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(1)}{}_{[8]}{}^{10,1}\end{matrix}

(5.46)

We will extend this chain of dualities infinitely to higher levels in Section
5.4
.
In the non-linear realisation, the duality relations are equivalence relations that only hold up to pure gauge terms.
So far, we have worked out these gauge terms for the duality relations up to level six.
These relations were written using the unfolded variables that are associated with each
E_{11}
field, and they hold exactly in the sense that they are gauge-covariant.
The difference between the duality relations found here and those of the non-linear realisation is that our proposed duality relations necessarily include extra fields that absorb all the gauge freedom.

5.3
Unfolding the field
A_{9,9,3}
at level seven

In this section we will unfold the second higher dual three-form
A_{9,9,3}
at level seven.
This will allow us to work out a first-order duality relation between this field and the first higher dual three-form
A_{9,3}
at level four.
The unfolded variables are

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}\;,\;\omega_{[9]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}\;,\;X_{[3]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}\;,\;C^{a[10],b[10],c[4]}\;,\;...

(5.47)

The first three unfolded equations are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}+h_{c}\,\omega_{[9]}{}^{c\langle a[9],b[3]\rangle}=0\;,

(5.48a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[9]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}+h_{c[7]}\,X_{[3]}{}^{a[10],c[7]b[3]}=0\;,

(5.48b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{[3]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}+h_{c[4]}\,C^{a[10],b[10],c[4]}=0\;,

(5.48c)

where the angled brackets denote the projection of the final twelve indices of
\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,3}
onto the irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9,3]
tableau.
As usual, the primary zero-form
C_{10,10,4}
belongs to the tower

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}(A_{9,9,3})=\big\{C^{(n)}_{10,10,4,1^{n}}\,\big|\;n\in\mathbb{N}\,\big\}=\big\{C^{(0)}_{10,10,4},C^{(1)}_{10,10,4,1},C^{(2)}_{10,10,4,1,1},\dots\big\}\;.

(5.49)

The unfolded equations are invariant under the gauge transformations

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}+h_{c}\,\alpha_{[8]}{}^{c\langle a[9],b[3]\rangle}\;,

(5.50a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[9]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}+h_{c[7]}\,\beta_{[2]}{}^{a[10],c[7]b[3]}\;,

(5.50b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[3]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[2]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}\;.

(5.50c)

As for any set of unfolded equations involving higher-degree forms, for each parameter there is a family of reducibility (gauge-for-gauge) transformations

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{[8-k]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[7-k]}{}^{a[9],b[3]}+h_{c}\,\alpha_{[7-k]}{}^{c\langle a[9],b[3]\rangle}\;,

(5.51a)

\displaystyle\delta\alpha_{[8-k]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[7-k]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}+h_{c[7]}\,\beta_{[1-k]}{}^{a[10],b[3]c[7]}\;,

(5.51b)

\displaystyle\delta\beta_{[2-k]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[1-k]}{}^{a[10],b[10]}\;,

(5.51c)

where
k=0,1,\dots,7
in (
5.51a
) and (
5.51b
), and
k=0,1
in (
5.51c
).
It is understood that a
p
-form with negative form degree is identically zero.

The irreducible fields and connections are given by

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9,3}

\displaystyle=A_{9,9,3}+\widehat{A}_{10,8,3}+\widehat{A}_{10,9,2}+\widehat{A}_{11,7,3}+\widehat{A}_{11,8,2}+\widehat{A}_{11,9,1}\;,

(5.52a)

\displaystyle\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,3}

\displaystyle=\omega_{10,9,3}+\omega_{10,10,2}+\omega_{11,8,3}+\omega_{11,9,2}+\omega_{11,10,1}\;,

(5.52b)

\displaystyle X_{[3]}{}^{10,10}

\displaystyle=X_{10,10,3}+X_{11,10,2}\;,

(5.52c)

The variable
e_{[9]}{}^{9,3}
contains the irreducible field
A_{9,9,3}
alongside five extra fields
\widehat{A}_{10,8,3}
,
\widehat{A}_{10,9,2}
,
\widehat{A}_{11,7,3}
,
\widehat{A}_{11,8,2}
, and
\widehat{A}_{11,9,1}
.
The irreducible gauge parameters are

\displaystyle\lambda_{[8]}{}^{9,3}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(1)}_{9,8,3}+\lambda^{(2)}_{9,9,2}+\lambda^{(3)}_{10,7,3}+\lambda^{(4)}_{10,8,2}+\lambda^{(5)}_{10,9,1}+\lambda^{(6)}_{11,6,3}+\lambda^{(7)}_{11,7,2}+\lambda^{(8)}_{11,8,1}+\lambda^{(9)}_{11,9}\;,

(5.53a)

\displaystyle\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{(1)}_{10,8,3}+\alpha^{(2)}_{10,9,2}+\alpha^{(3)}_{10,10,1}+\alpha^{(4)}_{11,7,3}+\alpha^{(5)}_{11,8,2}+\alpha^{(6)}_{11,9,1}+\alpha^{(7)}_{11,10}\;,

(5.53b)

\displaystyle\beta_{[2]}{}^{10,10}

\displaystyle=\beta^{(1)}_{10,10,2}+\beta^{(2)}_{11,10,1}\;.

(5.53c)

Now we will explain the role of each of each component.
The five extra fields in (
5.52a
) can be set to zero using the gauge parameters

\displaystyle\alpha^{(1)}_{10,8,3}\,,\,\alpha^{(2)}_{10,9,3}\,,\,\alpha^{(4)}_{11,7,3}\,,\,\alpha^{(5)}_{11,8,2}\,,\,\alpha^{(6)}_{11,9,1}\;.

(5.54)

After all the extra fields are eliminated, there will still exist some gauge symmetry in terms of the
\alpha^{(3)}_{10,10,1}
and
\alpha^{(7)}_{11,10}
parameters.
It seems that we are trying to gauge away two fields that do not exist.
However, the reducibility transformation

\displaystyle\delta\alpha_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[7]}{}^{a[10],b[3]}+h_{c[7]}\,\beta_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[3]c[7]}\;,

(5.55)

in (
5.51b
) tells us that
\alpha^{(3)}_{10,10,1}
and
\alpha^{(7)}_{11,10}
can both be shifted away using the components of the gauge-for-gauge parameter
\beta_{[1]}{}^{10,10}
in (
5.53c
).

In equation (
5.52b
) the connection
\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,3}
is decomposed into five irreducible components.
Two of them can be set to zero using the parameter
\beta_{[2]}{}^{10,10}
in (
5.50b
) and the other three are used to express
\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,3}
in terms of
(\mathrm{d}e)_{[10]}{}^{9,3}
.
Now notice that
(\mathrm{d}\omega)_{[10]}{}^{10,3}
has three components.
One of them vanishes as a result of the Bianchi identity
(\mathrm{d}^{2}e)_{[11]}{}^{9,3}=0
and the other two are used when
X_{[3]}{}^{10,10}
is expressed in terms of
(\mathrm{d}\omega)_{[10]}{}^{10,3}
.
In exactly the same way, one of the two components of
(\mathrm{d}X)_{[4]}{}^{10,10}
vanishes due to the Bianchi identity
(\mathrm{d}^{2}\omega)_{[11]}{}^{9,3}=0
, while the other one is used to express
C_{10,10,4}
in terms of
(\mathrm{d}X)_{[4]}{}^{10,10}
.
Consequently, after solving the unfolded equations and using the Bianchi identities and gauge symmetries, the primary zero-form
C_{10,10,4}
in (
5.48c
) can be expressed entirely in terms of the
A_{9,9,3}
field.

Recall that the components of
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}
either shift away the extra fields or are shifted away themselves using the reducibility parameter
\beta_{[1]}{}^{10,10}
.
Therefore, we find that all the gauge and gauge-for-gauge parameters account for each other except for the field
A_{9,9,3}
itself, the gauge parameters
\{\lambda^{(1)}_{9,8,3},\lambda^{(2)}_{9,9,2}\}
, and a small set of reducibility parameters
\{\lambda_{9,7,3},\lambda_{9,8,2},\lambda_{9,9,1},\dots\}
.

We now propose a first-order duality relation between the first higher dual three-form
A^{(1)}_{9,3}
at level four and the second higher dual three-form
A^{(2)}_{9,9,3}
at level seven.
As previously explained, the superscripts denote the number of higher dualisations.
Our duality relation takes the form

\displaystyle\omega^{(2)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{9}|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}\propto\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d}\,\omega^{(1)}_{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}|da_{1}\dots a_{9}}\;.

(5.56)

All the irreducible components of
e_{[9]}{}^{9,3}
in (
5.52a
) appear inside equation (
5.56
).
Importantly, this on-shell duality relation holds exactly.
Equation (
5.56
) is gauge-invariant when the gauge parameters
\beta_{[2]}{}^{10,10}
,
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}
, and
\alpha_{[2]}{}^{10}
for the connections in (
5.56
) are related by

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+\beta_{[a_{1}a_{2}||b_{1}\dots b_{10},|a_{3}\dots a_{9}]c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}\propto\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{c_{1}}\alpha_{c_{2}c_{3}|da_{1}\dots a_{9}}\;,

(5.57)

which is analogous to (
5.30
).
The constant of proportionality is the same as (
5.56
).
Recall that the previous duality relation (
4.97
) is gauge-invariant under the constraint (
4.108
) which forces
\alpha_{[2]}{}^{10}
to be pure gauge-for-gauge.
Under (
5.57
), this constraint now implies a further constraint on
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}
and
\beta_{[2]}{}^{10,10}
which leads to the first-order connection
\omega^{(2)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,3}
being gauge-invariant:

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+\beta_{[a_{1}a_{2}||b_{1}\dots b_{10},|a_{3}\dots a_{9}]c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}=0\;.

(5.58)

The gauge parameter constraints at higher levels will continue to enforce the gauge invariance of all the first-order on-shell duality relations.

Taking derivatives of (
5.56
) leads to the relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{d}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}A^{(1)}_{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}\;.

(5.59)

Working on-shell, the
A_{9,3}
equation of motion (
4.24
) is equivalent under (
5.59
) to

\displaystyle\partial^{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{6}c_{1}\dots c_{4}],}{}^{c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}=0\;.

(5.60)

In addition, antisymmetrising
d
with
b[10]
or
c[4]
causes the right-hand side of (
5.59
) to vanish, so the left-hand side is subject to some further on-shell constraints:

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial^{[b_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}A^{(2)}_{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],}{}^{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],}{}_{c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}=0\;,

\displaystyle\partial^{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{6}c_{1}\dots c_{4}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],}{}^{c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}=0\;.

(5.61)

Equations (
5.60
) and (
5.61
) are the equations of motion for the
A^{(2)}_{9,9,3}
field.

Solving the unfolded equations (
5.48a
), (
5.48b
) and (
5.48c
) for the primary zero-form, we find that
C_{10,10,4}
can be expressed up to a factor as the curvature tensor

\displaystyle C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{4}}=\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}\;.

(5.62)

We can now rewrite (
5.59
) as a relation between
C_{10,10,4}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(2)}_{9,9,3})
and
C_{10,4,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(1)}_{9,3})
:

\displaystyle C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}C_{b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}c_{4},d}\;.

(5.63)

As a result,
C_{10,10,4}
inherits the constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;,

\displaystyle\sigma_{2,3}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;,

(5.64)

and the remaining constraints are exchanged under (
5.63
) as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{10}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,2}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,3}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;\sigma_{1,3}(C_{10,4,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{2,3}(C_{10,4,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3}(C_{10,4,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3}(C_{10,4,1})=0\end{cases}

(5.65)

We can combine the zero-form relations (
5.63
) and (
4.22
) to obtain a new relation between
C_{10,10,4}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{9,9,3})
and
F^{(2)}_{4,1,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
:

\displaystyle C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d_{1}}\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d_{2}}F^{(2)}{}_{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}c_{4},d_{1},d_{2}}\;.

(5.66)

Taking a curl on the
c[4]
indices gives

\displaystyle\partial_{[e|}C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},|c_{1}\dots c_{4}]}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d_{1}}\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d_{2}}\partial_{[e}F^{(2)}{}_{c_{1}\dots c_{4}],d_{1},d_{2}}\;.

(5.67)

Equation (
3.96
) tells us that
F^{(3)}_{4,1,1,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
is really the GL
(11)
irreducible projection of the gradient of the adjacent zero-form
F^{(2)}_{4,1,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
, so equation (
5.67
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{[e|}C_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},|c_{1}\dots c_{4}]}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d_{1}}\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d_{2}}F^{(3)}{}_{[c_{1}\dots c_{4}|,d_{1},d_{2},|e]}=0\;.

(5.68)

The generalised Poincaré lemma
[
90
]
applied to (
5.68
) implies that
C_{10,10,4}
can be expressed as the curvature tensor (
5.62
).
This method will be useful when we consider fields at all higher levels since it allows us to express primary zero-forms and their gradients in terms of
E_{11}
fields without needing to solve an arbitrary number of unfolded equations.

Now we will present an equivalent method of obtaining the linearised equations of motion, i.e. the higher trace constraints.
On-shell, all the zero-forms
F^{(n)}_{4,1,\dots,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
are irreducible Lorentz representations, and as a result the properties of
F^{(2)}_{4,1,1}
are exchanged under (
5.66
) with constraints on the curvature tensor
C_{10,10,4}
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{10}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,2}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,3}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{2,3}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3}(F_{4,1,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{1,2}(F_{4,1,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{1,3}(F_{4,1,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{2,3}(F_{4,1,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2}(F_{4,1,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3}(F_{4,1,1})=0\end{cases}

(5.69)

Our notation
\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j}
and
\sigma_{i,j}
is the same as in (
5.36
).
Thus the postulated relation (
5.66
) leads to the higher trace constraints:

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{10}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,4})=0\;.

(5.70)

When
C_{10,10,4}
is expressed as the curvature tensor (
5.62
), these trace constraints are equivalent to the equations of motion (
5.60
) and (
5.61
) for
A^{(2)}_{9,9,3}
.
Even if we unfold off-shell without these constraints, it is immediate to see that
C_{10,10,4}
is invariant under the gauge transformation

\displaystyle\delta A_{a_{1}\dots a_{9},b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}=\left[\partial_{[b_{1}|}\lambda^{(1)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{9},|b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+\partial_{[c_{1}|}\lambda^{(2)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{9},b_{1}\dots b_{9},|c_{2}c_{3}]}\right]_{9,9,3}\;,

(5.71)

where
[\,\dots]_{9,9,3}
denotes a projection onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9,9,3]
tableau.

Working backwards from the third-order curvature relation (
5.59
), we can integrate
b[10]
to introduce an arbitrary
\Xi_{8|10,3}
tensor, and then we can shift it as

\displaystyle\Xi_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}\;\longmapsto\;\Xi_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+9\,\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}A^{(1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{8}d,c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}\;,

(5.72)

leading to a second-order relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{9},|c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}+\partial_{[c_{1}|}\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{9}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},|c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}\hskip 85.35826pt

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(10\,\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[d}A^{(1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}c_{3}c_{4}]}\right)\;,

(5.73)

where we have used the Poincaré lemma again on
c[4]
to make the curl on these indices explicit in every term.
Integrating on
c[4]
now gives us the first-order relation (
5.56
) in the form

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+\partial_{[b_{1}}

\displaystyle\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{9}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(10\,\partial_{[d}A^{(1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{9}],c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}+\partial_{[c_{1}}\Theta_{c_{2}c_{3}]|db_{1}\dots b_{9}}\right)\;.

(5.74)

The irreducible components of
\Theta_{2|10}
are identified with the two extra fields in (
4.10a
) and the irreducible components of
\Xi_{8|10,3}
are identified either with the set of extra fields in (
5.52a
) or with the components of
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}
that can be set to zero using the gauge-for-gauge parameter
\beta_{[1]}{}^{10,10}
in (
5.51c
).

The duality relation (
5.56
) holds exactly.
However, it can be written as an equivalence relation between the first terms on the left-hand and right-hand sides of (
5.74
).
The precise meaning of this equivalence relation is explained in (
5.74
) which is found by integrating either the equation of motion of
A^{(1)}_{9,3}
or that of
A^{(2)}_{9,9,3}
.

5.4
Unfolding and duality relations at higher levels

In this section, we restrict our attention to the irreducible fields
\big\{A^{(n)}_{9,\dots,9,3}\,,A^{(n)}_{9,\dots,9,6}\,,h^{(n)}_{9,\dots,9,8,1}\big\}
in (
2
) whose blocks of antisymmetric indices are no larger than nine, and we propose first-order duality relations between them at arbitrarily high levels.
As discussed at lower levels, we found that they are relations between the first-order connections associated with each field.

Unfolding higher dual three-forms.

In order to unfold the
n^{\text{th}}
higher dual
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
in
E_{11}
at level
3n+1
, we introduce the following variables:

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}\;,\;\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},3}\;,\;\dots\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{n-1},3}\;,\;X_{[3]}{}^{10^{n}}\;,\;C^{10^{n},4}\;,\;\dots

(5.75)

Schematically, the first two unfolded equations can be written as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}+h_{1}\,\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.76a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}+h_{1}\,X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},3}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.76b)

and they are invariant under the gauge symmetries

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}+h_{1}\,\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}\;,

(5.77a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}+h_{1}\,\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},3}\;,

(5.77b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},3}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},3}\;.

(5.77c)

The primary zero-form
C_{10^{n},4}
is the first in the tower

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}(A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3})

\displaystyle=\{C^{(m)}_{10^{n},4,1^{m}}\,|\;m\in\mathbb{N}\,\}=\{C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4},C^{(1)}_{10^{n},4,1},C^{(2)}_{10^{n},4,1,1},\dots\}\;.

(5.78)

The first variable in the tower
e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}
decomposes into irreducible components as

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}=A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}+\widehat{A}_{10,9^{n-1},8,3}+\widehat{A}_{10,9^{n-1},2}+\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-2},7,3}+\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-2},8,2}+\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-1},1}\;,

(5.79)

where we can see the higher dual field
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
alongside five extra fields.
Upon solving the first unfolded equation, the second variable
\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}
will be given in terms of derivatives of these six fields.

When two fields are related by electromagnetic duality, there must be a bijection between their zero-form modules.
For example, the six-form in
E_{11}
at level two is the magnetic dual of the three-form at level one, and the zero-forms
F^{(n)}_{4,1,\dots,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
are related to the zero-forms
F^{(n)}_{7,1,\dots,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{6})
via (
3.5
) and (
3.5
) that we reproduce here:

\displaystyle F^{(n)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{7},c_{1},\dots,c_{n}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{7}}{}^{b_{1}\dots b_{4}}F^{(n)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{4},c_{1},\dots,c_{n}}\;,\qquad n=0,1,2,\dots

(5.80)

These relations are different in the case of higher (gradient) dualisations.
For example,
A_{9,3}
is the first higher dual three-form field, and the zero-forms
C^{(n)}_{10,4,1^{n}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{9,3})
are related to the zero-forms
F^{(n+1)}_{4,1^{n+1}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
by the shifted relations

\displaystyle C^{(n)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{4},c^{1},\dots,c^{n}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}F^{(n+1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{4},c_{1},\dots,c_{n},d}\;.

(5.81)

This is not a bijection since
F_{4}\equiv F^{(0)}_{4}
does not correspond to any zero-form in
\mathscr{T}(A_{9,3})
.

As explained in
[
23
]
, considering only the three-form sector for the sake of definiteness, we need all the zero-forms in
\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
at a point in space-time
x_{0}
together with the infinite tower of unfolded equations (
3.91
), (
3.94
) and (
3.95
) in order to reconstruct an on-shell dynamical three-form field in some open neighbourhood around
x_{0}
using the Taylor expansion

\displaystyle A_{a[3]}(x)=A_{a[3]}(x_{0})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{1\over n!}(x-x_{0})^{b_{1}}\dots(x-x_{0})^{b_{n}}F^{(n-1)}_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{n})}(x_{0})\;.

(5.82)

If we were to write down a Taylor expansion for
A_{9,3}
analogous to (
5.82
), the coefficients that are usually given in terms of the tensors
\{C^{(0)}_{10,4},C^{(1)}_{10,4,1},C^{(2)}_{10,4,1,1},\dots\}
would instead be given in terms of
\{F^{(1)}_{4,1},F^{(2)}_{4,1,1},F^{(3)}_{4,1,1,1},\dots\}
.
Notice that the zero-form
F^{(0)}_{4}
in the linear term of the three-form expansion is no longer present.
Thus the first higher dual
A_{9,3}
describes the on-shell dynamical three-form beyond first-order, i.e.​ at long distances.
This truncation only omits one of the zero-forms in
\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
and the field equations can still be reconstructed by integrating Bianchi identities.

Duality relations for higher dual three-forms.

We have already found a duality relation between
A^{(2)}_{9,9,3}
and
A^{(1)}_{9,3}
in equation (
5.56
) and now we propose, in the context of the unfolded formalism, an infinite number of first-order on-shell duality relations for the entire three-form sector.
In particular, we relate pairs of adjacent higher dual fields
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
and
A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},3}
for
n>2
.
These higher relations have a different form to (
5.56
) between the first and second higher dual three-forms.
The duality relations at all higher levels are

\displaystyle\omega^{(n)}{}_{a[9]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}\;\propto\;\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p}\,\omega^{(n-1)}{}_{a[9]|pc[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}\;,

(5.83)

where
\omega^{(n)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}
are the first-order connections associated with
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
in (
5.76a
).

We require that our duality relations are gauge-covariant, so taking the gauge transformation of both sides leads to a relation between
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9,3}
,
\beta_{[8]}{}^{10,10,3}
,
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,3}
, and
\beta_{[2]}{}^{10,10}
for
n=3
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b[10],c[9],d[3]}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||b[10],|a_{9}]c[9],d[3]}\hskip 142.26378pt\\
\propto\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p}\left(\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|pc[9],d[3]}+\beta_{[a_{1}a_{2}||pc[9],|a_{3}\dots a_{9}]d[3]}\right)\;.\end{split}

(5.84)

For the higher duality relations with
n>3
, we have the constraints

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||b[10],|a_{9}]c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}\hskip 85.35826pt\\
\propto\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p}\left(\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|pc[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||pc[9],|a_{9}]d^{1}[9],d^{2}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}\right)\;.\end{split}

(5.85)

Thus for all our duality relations to be gauge-covariant, we need to impose an infinite tower of gauge parameter constraints for
n\geq 3
, each of which follows from the previous one:

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||b[10],|a_{9}]c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[3]}=0\;.

(5.86)

These constraints create more field degrees of freedom, and they force every connection
\omega^{(n)}
to be gauge-invariant.
Consequently, we have an infinite set of extra fields that appear explicitly in the tower of duality relations.

Taking derivatives of (
5.83
) leads to the gauge-invariant relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\partial_{c}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}\;.

(5.87)

Now we want to show that taking appropriate traces leads to the equations of motion for each field.
For this we suppose that the equations of motion for
A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},3}
are

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}a^{j}_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{10}a^{j}_{10}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n-1}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n-1}_{2}\dots a^{n-1}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.88)

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}b_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{4}b_{4}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n-1}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n-1}_{2}\dots a^{n-1}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.89)

for all
i
and
j
with
1\leq i<j\leq n-1
.
These equations generalise (
4.99
), (
5.60
), and (
5.61
) that we found earlier for low values of
n
.
From this,
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
inherits

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}a^{j}_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{10}a^{j}_{10}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.90)

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}b_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{4}b_{4}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.91)

for
2\leq i<j\leq n
.
Antisymmetrising
c
with
a^{i}[10]
in (
5.87
) for
2\leq i\leq n
leads to

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{1}_{1}a^{j}_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{1}_{10}a^{j}_{10}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}=0\;,

(5.92)

while antisymmetrising
c
with
b[4]
leads to

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{1}_{1}b_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{1}_{4}b_{4}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}=0\;.

(5.93)

Thus for
n>2
we have shown inductively that the equations of motion of
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
are (
5.90
) and (
5.91
) for
1\leq i<j\leq n
, and that they all follow from the infinite chain of dualities (
5.83
).

Reformulation in terms of zero-forms.

The discussion above is quite cumbersome.
Here we will express everything in terms of the zero-forms in the unfolded formalism, and this will once again give us extremely compact forms of the curvature relations and equations of motion.
We introduce a zero-form relation between
F^{(n)}_{4,1^{n}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
and
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3})
analogous to equations (
4.22
) and (
5.66
) at lower levels:

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[4]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}F^{(n)}{}_{b[4],d_{1},\dots,d_{n}}\;.

(5.94)

This is one of an infinite number of shifted zero-form relations

\displaystyle C^{(m)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[4],c_{1},\dots,c_{m}}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}F^{(n+m)}{}_{b[4],d_{1},\dots,d_{n},c_{1},\dots,c_{m}}\;.

(5.95)

As a result, if we write down a Taylor expansion for
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
analogous to (
5.82
), the coefficients that are usually given in terms of
\{C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4},C^{(1)}_{10^{n},4,1},C^{(2)}_{10^{n},4,1,1},\dots\}
will instead be given in terms of
\{F^{(n)}_{4,1^{n}},F^{(n+1)}_{4,1^{n+1}},F^{(n+2)}_{4,1^{n+2}},\dots\}
.
The first
n
zero-forms
\{F^{(0)}_{4},F^{(1)}_{4,1},\dots,F^{(n-1)}_{4,1^{n-1}}\}
do not appear in the Taylor expansion of
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
around a point in space-time.
Therefore, higher dual fields
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
for increasing
n
describe the original three-form at higher and higher orders, meaning at longer and longer distances.
The same is true for the higher dual six-forms
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
and gravitons
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
.
As before, only a finite set of zero-forms is omitted, and integrating the Bianchi identities leads to all the original equations of motion.

Returning to the zero-form relation (
5.94
), taking a curl on the
b[4]
indices gives

\displaystyle\partial_{[e|}C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],|b_{1}\dots b_{4}]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}\partial_{[e}F^{(n)}{}_{b_{1}\dots b_{4}],d_{1},\dots,d_{n}}\;,

(5.96)

but the zero-form
F^{(n+1)}_{4,1^{n+1}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
is irreducible, so (
5.96
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{[e|}C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],|b_{1}\dots b_{4}]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}F^{(n+1)}{}_{[b_{1}\dots b_{4}|,d_{1},\dots,d_{n},|e]}=0\;.

(5.97)

The generalised Poincaré lemma implies that
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}
can be expressed as the curvature tensor

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[4]}=\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}b_{4}]}

(5.98)

for the
n^{\text{th}}
higher dual three-form
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
.
This is precisely what one would find by solving the first
n+1
unfolded equations, but here we have finished in one step.
It is immediate to see that this curvature is invariant under

\displaystyle\delta A^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}=\left[\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\lambda^{(1)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n-1}[9],|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{9}],b[3]}+\partial_{[b_{1}|}\lambda^{(2)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b_{2}b_{3}]}\right]_{9^{n},3}\;,

(5.99)

where
[\,\dots]_{9^{n},3}
denotes a projection onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9^{n},3]
tableau.

Working on-shell, the zero-forms
F^{(n)}_{4,1^{n}}
are all irreducible Lorentz tensors.
The irreducibility properties of
F^{(n)}_{4,1^{n}}
are exchanged under equation (
5.94
) as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{4}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{i,j}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{i,n+1}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{i+1,j+1}(F_{4,1^{n}})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{1,i+1}(F_{4,1^{n}})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{i+1,j+1}(F_{4,1^{n}})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{1,i+1}(F_{4,1^{n}})=0\end{cases}

(5.100)

where
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
.
The primary zero-form
C_{10^{n},4}
now obeys the higher trace constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;,\qquad(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{4}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;,\qquad 1\leq i<j\leq n\;.

(5.101)

Thus the irreducibility properties of
F^{(n)}_{4,1^{n}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{3})
led to an extremely compact form (
5.100
) of the linearised equations of motion (
5.90
) and (
5.91
), where
C_{10^{n},4}
is the curvature (
5.98
).

The zero-form relations (
5.94
) for adjacent values of
n
imply a new relation between the primary zero-forms
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3})
and
C^{(1)}_{10^{n-1},4,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},3})
:

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],a^{2}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[4]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d}C^{(1)}{}_{a^{2}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[4],d}\;.

(5.102)

When these zero-forms are expressed in terms of the original fields using (
5.98
), we find that (
5.102
) reproduces the curvature relation (
5.87
).
Under (
5.102
), the zero-form
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}
inherits from
C^{(1)}_{10^{n-1},4,1}
the constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},4})=(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{4}(C_{10^{n},4})=\sigma_{i,j}(C_{10^{n},4})=\sigma_{i,n+1}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;,

(5.103)

for
2\leq i<j\leq n
.
The remaining constraints are exchanged as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,i})^{10}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,n+1})^{4}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,i}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\\
\sigma_{1,n+1}(C_{10^{n},4})=0\;\;\end{cases}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\;\;\sigma_{i-1,n+1}(C_{10^{n-1},4,1})=0\\
\;\;\sigma_{n,n+1}(C_{10^{n-1},4,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{i-1,n+1}(C_{10^{n-1},4,1})=0\\
\;\;\mathrm{Tr}_{n,n+1}(C_{10^{n-1},4,1})=0\end{cases}

(5.104)

Integrating curvature relations.

Working backwards from the higher curvature relations (
5.87
), we can integrate
a^{2}[10]
, introduce an arbitrary tensor
\Xi_{8|10,9^{n-2},3}
and impose the shift

\displaystyle\Xi_{a^{2}[8]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}\;\longmapsto\;\Xi_{a^{2}[8]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}+9\,\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[8]c,a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}\;,

(5.105)

to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{3}|}\partial_{[a^{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9]],a^{2}[9],|a^{3}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}+\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{3}|}\partial_{[a^{2}}\Xi_{a^{2}[8]]|a^{1}[10],|a^{3}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\left(10\,\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{3}}\partial_{[c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[9]],a^{3}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}\right)\;.

(5.106)

Integrating
a^{3}[10]
, we introduce an arbitrary tensor
\Theta_{8|10,9^{n-3},3}
on the right-hand side:

\displaystyle\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{4}|}\partial_{[a^{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9]],a^{2}[9],a^{3}[9],|a^{4}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{4}|}\partial_{[a^{2}}\Xi_{a^{2}[8]]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],|a^{4}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\big(10\,\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{4}|}\partial_{[c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[9]],a^{3}[9],|a^{4}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{n}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{4}|}\partial_{[a^{3}}\Theta_{a^{3}[8]]|ca^{2}[9],|a^{4}[9]],\dots,|a^{n}[9]],b[3]]}\big)\;.

(5.107)

The reducible tensor
\Xi_{8|10,9^{n-2},3}
contains the extra fields

\displaystyle\{\widehat{A}_{10,9^{n-2},8,3},\widehat{A}_{10,9^{n-1},2},\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-2},7,3},\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-2},8,2},\widehat{A}_{11,9^{n-1},1}\}

(5.108)

that are associated with the
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
field, while
\Theta_{8|10,9^{n-3},3}
contains those that are associated with the
A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},3}
field.

Integrating the
a^{4}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10]
columns produces a sequence of tensors, each of which is absorbed into the previous one since we can swap all these columns with each other and also with
a^{2}[10]
and
a^{3}[10]
.
The result of this repeated integration is

\displaystyle\partial_{[b|}\partial_{[a^{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9]],a^{2}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b[3]]}+\partial_{[b}\partial_{[a^{2}}\Xi_{a^{2}[8]]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b[3]]}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\big(10\,\partial_{[b|}\partial_{[c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[9]],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b[3]]}+\partial_{[b|}\partial_{[a^{3}}\Theta_{a^{3}[8]]|ca^{2}[9],a^{4}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b[3]]}\big)\;.

(5.109)

Integrating one final time and introducing an arbitrary
\Upsilon_{2|10,9^{n-1}}
tensor, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{[a^{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9]],a^{2}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}+\partial_{[a^{2}}\Xi_{a^{2}[8]]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}+\partial_{[b}\Upsilon_{b[2]]|a^{1}[10],a^{2}[9],\dots,\dots,a^{n}[9]}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\big(10\,\partial_{[c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[9]],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}+\partial_{[a^{3}}\Theta_{a^{3}[8]]|ca^{2}[9],a^{4}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[3]}\big)\;.

(5.110)

These duality relations would have been equivalence equations in the
E_{11}
non-linear realisation meaning that they would only hold up to certain pure gauge terms.
By integrating the equations of motion, we have found relations that hold exactly when the gauge parameters are subject to certain constraints.
The gauge freedom is absorbed by extra fields.
This is an elaboration of (both the computation and the result of) the duality relations in
equation (3.5.14) of reference
[
23
]
, but now the extra fields
\Theta_{8|10,9^{n-3},3}
are explicit.
Every term in (
5.110
) needs to be projected onto the GL
(11)
-irreducible representation associated with the
\mathbb{Y}[10,9,\dots,9,3]
diagram.

The first-order connections
\omega^{(n)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}
and
\omega^{(n-1)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-3},3}
in the duality relations (
5.83
) are variables that come from the unfolded formalism.
Notice that
\Xi_{8|10,9^{n-2},3}
in (
5.110
) has the same structure as the gauge parameter
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}
in (
5.77a
).
Some components of
\Xi_{8|10,9^{n-2},3}
are identified with the extra fields in
e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},3}
and the others are identified with the components of
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}
that can be shifted away with the gauge-for-gauge parameter
\beta_{[7]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},2}
.
Some components of
\beta_{[7]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},2}
may subsequently be shifted away using a gauge-for-gauge-for-gauge parameter
\gamma_{[6]}{}^{10^{3},9^{n-4},3}
, and so on.
Looking back at (
5.110
), it is unclear where (or if)
\Upsilon_{2|10,9^{n-2}}
originates in the unfolded formalism, so it is not included in the duality relation (
5.83
).

In summary, equations (
5.56
) and (
5.83
) extend the set of duality
relations (
5.20
) to include the entire three-form sector.

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\vskip 2.84526ptF_{4}\vskip-8.53581pt&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(1)}{}_{[3]}{}^{10}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(2)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,3}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(3)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,3}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(4)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,9,3}&\longleftrightarrow\hskip 8.53581pt\cdots\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&&&&&&\\
F_{7}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(1)}{}_{[6]}{}^{10}&&&&&&&\end{matrix}\hskip-28.45274pt

(5.111)

Unfolding higher dual six-forms.

We will now consider the six-form sector of the theory.
Our analysis will be similar to that of the three-form sector, so we will not dwell on all details.
To unfold the
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
field in
E_{11}
at level
3n+2
, we introduce the following variables:

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},6}\;,\;\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},6}\;,\;\dots\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{n-1},6}\;,\;X_{[6]}{}^{10^{n}}\;,\;C^{10^{n},7}\;,\;\dots

(5.112)

Schematically, the first two unfolded equations are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},6}+h_{1}\,\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.113a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}+h_{1}\,X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},6}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.113b)

and they are invariant under the gauge symmetries

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},6}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{9^{n-1},6}+h_{1}\,\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}\;,

(5.114a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}+h_{1}\,\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},6}\;,

(5.114b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},6}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},6}\;.

(5.114c)

The primary zero-form
C_{10^{n},7}
is the first in the tower

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}(A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6})

\displaystyle=\{C^{(m)}_{10^{n},7,1^{m}}\,|\;m\in\mathbb{N}\,\}=\{C^{(0)}_{10^{n},7},C^{(1)}_{10^{n},7,1},C^{(2)}_{10^{n},7,1,1},\dots\}\;.

(5.115)

Duality relation between
A^{(2)}_{9,9,6}
and
A^{(1)}_{9,6}
.

Before proceeding to arbitrarily high levels, it is useful to consider the first-order duality relation between the first and second higher dual six-forms in terms of their first-order connections:

\displaystyle\omega^{(2)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{9}|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{6}}\propto\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{d}\,\omega^{(1)}_{c_{1}\dots c_{6}|da_{1}\dots a_{9}}\;.

(5.116)

Requiring this to be gauge-invariant leads to a gauge parameter relation analogous to (
5.57
).
The constraint associated with the previous duality relation (
5.10
) told us that the parameter
\alpha_{[5]}{}^{10}
is pure gauge-for-gauge, leading to the next constraint associated with (
5.116
):

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{6}}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{5}||b_{1}\dots b_{10},|a_{6}\dots a_{9}]c_{1}\dots c_{6}}=0\;.

(5.117)

This gauge parameter constraint allows the extra fields to appear explicitly in (
5.116
).

Taking derivatives of the duality relation (
5.116
) leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}\dots c_{7}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\partial_{d}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}A^{(1)}_{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}\dots c_{7}]}\;,

(5.118)

and taking appropriate traces leads either to the equations of motion for
A^{(1)}_{9,6}
that were found to be (
5.12
), or to the following equations of motion for the
A^{(2)}_{9,9,6}
field:

\displaystyle\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial^{[a_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],}{}^{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],}{}_{b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}=0\;,

(5.119)

\displaystyle\partial^{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}b_{3}c_{1}\dots c_{7}],}{}^{c_{2}\dots c_{7}]}=0\;.

(5.120)

We can also reformulate these equations in terms of the zero-forms in the unfolded formalism.
The techniques used to do this for the three-form sector show that
C^{(0)}_{10,10,7}
can be expressed as the curvature of
A^{(2)}_{9,9,6}
.
The curvature relation (
5.118
) then becomes

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{7}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}C^{(1)}{}_{b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{7},d}\;.

(5.121)

and the higher trace constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{10}(C_{10,10,7})=0\;,\qquad(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,7})=0\;,\qquad(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3})^{4}(C_{10,10,7})=0\;,

(5.122)

are equivalent to the linearised equations of motion for
A^{(2)}_{9,9,6}
.

As always, we can integrate up the equations of motion to obtain first-order relations with extra fields appearing explicitly.
Writing (
5.121
) in terms of the gauge potentials, integrating this equation twice leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}A^{(2)}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}\dots c_{6}}+\partial_{[b_{1}}

\displaystyle\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{9}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{6}}

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{d}\left(10\,\partial_{[d}A^{(1)}_{b_{1}\dots b_{9}],c_{1}\dots c_{6}}+\partial_{[c_{1}}\Theta_{c_{2}\dots c_{6}]|db_{1}\dots b_{9}}\right)\;.

(5.123)

The irreducible components of
\Xi_{8|10,6}
and
\Theta_{5|10}
include the extra fields associated with
A^{(2)}_{9,9,6}
and
A^{(1)}_{9,6}
, respectively.

Duality relations for higher dual six-forms.

In exactly the same way that we were led to (
5.83
) in the three-form sector, here we propose first-order on-shell duality relations between adjacent higher dual six-form fields
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
and
A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},6}
for
n>2
:

\displaystyle\omega^{(n)}{}_{a[9]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[6]}\;\propto\;\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p}\,\omega^{(n-1)}{}_{a[9]|pc[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[6]}\;.

(5.124)

The first-order connections
\{\omega^{(n)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}\}
are the unfolded variables that appear in (
5.113a
).
For these duality relations to be gauge-invariant, we need to impose the constraints

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[6]}+\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||b[10],|a_{9}]c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[6]}=0\;.

(5.125)

These are essentially the same as the constraints for the three-form sector.

Taking derivatives leads to the gauge-invariant relations

\displaystyle\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}\hskip 170.71652pt

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\partial_{c}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}\;.

(5.126)

and taking appropriate traces leads to the equations of motion for the
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
field

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}a^{j}_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{10}a^{j}_{10}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.127)

\displaystyle\eta^{a^{i}_{1}b_{1}}\dots\eta^{a^{i}_{7}b_{7}}\,\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.128)

for
1\leq i<j\leq n
.

Reformulation in terms of zero-forms.

As we did in the three-form sector, we relate the primary zero-form
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{9^{n},3})
to the zero-form
F^{(n)}_{7,1^{n}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{6})
through the relation

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[7]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}F^{(n)}{}_{b[7],d_{1},\dots,d_{n}}\;,

(5.129)

which mirrors (
5.94
).
This is one of an infinite number of shifted zero-form relations

\displaystyle C^{(m)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[7],c_{1},\dots,c_{m}}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d_{1}}\dots\varepsilon_{a^{n}[10]}{}^{d_{n}}F^{(n+m)}{}_{b[7],d_{1},\dots,d_{n},c_{1},\dots,c_{m}}\;.

(5.130)

Taking a curl of (
5.129
) on the
b[7]
indices, using Lorentz irreducibility of
F^{(n+1)}_{7,1^{n+1}}\in\mathscr{T}(A_{6})
, and applying the generalised Poincaré lemma, we find that
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},4}
is the curvature tensor

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[7]}=\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}A^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}

(5.131)

for the
n^{\text{th}}
higher dual six-form
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
.
It is immediate to see that this is invariant under

\displaystyle\delta A^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[6]}=\left[\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\lambda^{(1)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n-1}[9],|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{9}],b[6]}+\partial_{[b_{1}|}\lambda^{(2)}{}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b_{2}\dots b_{6}]}\right]_{9^{n},6}\;,

(5.132)

where
[\,\dots]_{9^{n},6}
denotes a projection onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9^{n},6]
tableau.

Working on-shell, the zero-forms
F^{(n)}_{7,1^{n}}
are all irreducible Lorentz tensors.
The properties of
F^{(n)}_{7,1^{n}}
are exchanged with constraints on
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},7}
under (
5.129
) as in (
5.100
) where all the fours are replaced by sevens.
Therefore,
C_{10^{n},7}
obeys higher trace constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},7})=0\;,\qquad(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{7}(C_{10^{n},7})=0\;,\qquad 1\leq i<j\leq n\;,

(5.133)

which are equivalent to the linearised equations of motion for all higher
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
fields.

Considering equation (
5.129
) for adjacent values of
n
, we find a zero-form relation between
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},7}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6})
and
C^{(1)}_{10^{n-1},7,1}\in\mathscr{T}(A^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},6})
which takes the form

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],a^{2}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[7]}=\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d}C^{(1)}{}_{a^{2}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[7],d}\;.

(5.134)

Under this relation,
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},7}
inherits from
C^{(1)}_{10^{n-1},7,1}
the constraints

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},7})=(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{7}(C_{10^{n},7})=\sigma_{i,j}(C_{10^{n},7})=\sigma_{i,n+1}(C_{10^{n},7})=0\;,

(5.135)

for
2\leq i<j\leq n
, and the remaining constraints are exchanged as in (
5.104
) where all the fours are once again replaced by sevens.

Integrating curvature relations.

Repeated integration of the curvature relation (
5.126
) leads to a first-order on-shell duality relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[a^{1}}A^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9]],a^{2}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[6]}+\partial_{[a^{2}}\Xi_{a^{2}[8]]|a^{1}[10],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[6]}+\partial_{[b}\Upsilon_{b[5]]|a^{1}[10],a^{2}[9],\dots,\dots,a^{n}[9]}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{c}\big(10\,\partial_{[c}A^{(n-1)}_{a^{2}[9]],a^{3}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b[6]}+\partial_{[a^{3}}\Theta_{a^{3}[8]]|ca^{2}[9],a^{4}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[6]}\big)\;,

(5.136)

featuring arbitrary tensors
\Xi_{8|10,9^{n-2},6}
,
\Theta_{8|10,9^{n-3},6}
, and
\Upsilon_{5|10,9^{n-1}}
.
Note that (
5.136
) must be projected onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9,\dots,9,6]
tableau.
The tensors
\Xi
and
\Theta
are clearly identified with extra fields since they have the same symmetry types as the
\alpha
parameters in the unfolded equations, but once again it is not known if the irreducible fields in
\Upsilon_{5|10,9^{n}}
originate from the unfolded equations and gauge symmetries.

Duality relations between
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},3}
and
A^{(n)}_{9^{n},6}
.

It is now straightforward to obtain relations between the
n^{\text{th}}
higher dual three-form and six-form fields for
n\geq 2
.
They constitute the rungs of the ladder in the diagram below.

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\cdots&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(n)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},3}&\longleftrightarrow\hskip 8.53581pt\cdots\\
&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&&&&\\
\cdots&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(n)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},6}&\longleftrightarrow\hskip 8.53581pt\cdots\end{matrix}

(5.137)

These duality relations take the form

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{(n)}{}_{[a_{1}|a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}]}\;\propto\;\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{4}}{}^{b_{1}\dots b_{7}}\,\widetilde{\omega}^{(n)}{}_{[b_{1}|b_{2}\dots b_{7}]}\;,

(5.138)

where the definitions of
\widetilde{\omega}^{(n)}_{1|3}
and
\widetilde{\omega}^{(n)}_{1|6}
depend on the parity of
n
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{(2m)}{}_{a|b[k]}:=\hbox{}

\displaystyle\omega^{(2m)}{}_{d[9]|c[9]a,}{}^{c[9],d[9],}{}_{e^{1}[9],}{}^{e^{1}[9],}{}_{\dots,e^{m-2}[9],}{}^{e^{m-2}[9],}{}_{b[k]}\;,

(5.139)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{(2m-1)}{}_{a|b[k]}:=\hbox{}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{e[10]}{}_{a}\,\omega^{(2m-1)}{}_{c[9]|e[10],}{}^{c[9],}{}_{d^{1}[9],}{}^{d^{1}[9],}{}_{\dots,d^{m-2}[9],}{}^{d^{m-2}[9],}{}_{b[k]}\;.

(5.140)

Setting
k=3
or
k=6
gives the appropriate definition for each sector.

We now have an infinite ladder of first-order on-shell duality relations.
One of the rails of the ladder is populated by higher duality relations for the three-form sector: (
4.97
), (
5.56
) and (
5.83
).
The other rail of the ladder is populated by those of the six-form sector: (
5.10
), (
5.116
) and (
5.124
).
Lastly, the rungs of the ladder are populated by electromagnetic dualities: (
3.5
), (
5.20
), and (
5.138
).
This is summarised as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\vskip 2.84526ptF_{4}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(1)}{}_{[3]}{}^{10}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(2)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,3}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(3)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,3}&\longleftrightarrow&\vskip 2.84526pt\omega^{(4)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,9,3}&\longleftrightarrow\hskip 8.53581pt\cdots\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&&\vskip 2.84526pt\Big\updownarrow&\\
F_{7}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(1)}{}_{[6]}{}^{10}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(2)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,6}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(3)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,6}&\longleftrightarrow&\omega^{(4)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,9,9,6}&\longleftrightarrow\hskip 8.53581pt\cdots\end{matrix}\hskip-28.45274pt

(5.141)

Unfolding higher dual gravitons.

Lastly, we will sketch the unfolding of the gravitational sector of the theory at all levels.
In order to unfold the second higher dual graviton
h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1}
in
E_{11}
at level nine, we introduce a tower of variables

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9,8,1}\;,\;\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,8,1}\;,\;X_{[8]}{}^{10,10,1}\;,\;Y_{[1]}{}^{10,10,9}\;,\;C^{10,10,9,2}\;,\;\dots

(5.142)

The first four unfolded equations are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{a[9],b[8],c}+h_{d}\,\omega_{[9]}{}^{d\langle a[9],b[8],c\rangle}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.143a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[9]}{}^{a[10],b[8],c}+h_{d[2]}\,X_{[8]}{}^{a[10],d[2]\langle b[8],c\rangle}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.143b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b[10],c}+h_{d[8]}\,Y_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[10],d[8]c}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.143c)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[10],c[9]}+h_{d[2]}\,C^{a[10],b[10],c[9],d[2]}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.143d)

where angled brackets denote projection onto the obvious irreducible Young tableaux, and these equations are invariant under the gauge symmetries

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{a[9],b[8],c}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{a[9],b[8],c}+h_{d}\,\alpha_{[8]}{}^{d\langle a[9],b[8],c\rangle}\;,

(5.144a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[9]}{}^{a[10],b[8],c}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b[8],c}-h_{d[2]}\,\beta_{[7]}{}^{a[10],d[2]\langle b[8],c\rangle}\;,

(5.144b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[8]}{}^{a[10],b[10],c}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[7]}{}^{a[10],b[10],c}-h_{d[8]}\,\gamma^{a[10],b[10],d[8]c}\;,

(5.144c)

\displaystyle\delta Y_{[1]}{}^{a[10],b[10],c[9]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\gamma^{a[10],b[10],c[9]}\;.

(5.144d)

Moreover, in order to unfold the
n^{\text{th}}
higher dual graviton
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
in
E_{11}
at level
3n+3
, where
n\geq 3
, we introduce a tower of variables beginning with

\displaystyle e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},8,1}\;,\;\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},8,1}\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{3},9^{n-4},8,1}\;,\;\dots

(5.145)

Schematically, the first two unfolded equations are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},8,1}+h_{1}\,\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.146a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}+h_{1}\,X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},8,1}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(5.146b)

and they are invariant under

\displaystyle\delta e_{[9]}{}^{9^{n-1},8,1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\lambda_{[8]}{}^{9^{n-1},8,1}+h_{1}\,\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}\;,

(5.147a)

\displaystyle\delta\omega_{[9]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,9^{n-2},8,1}+h_{1}\,\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},8,1}\;,

(5.147b)

\displaystyle\delta X_{[9]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},8,1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\beta_{[8]}{}^{10^{2},9^{n-3},8,1}\;.

(5.147c)

The tower (
5.145
) continues as

\displaystyle\dots\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{n-2},9,8,1}\;,\;X_{[9]}{}^{10^{n-1},8,1}\;,\;X_{[8]}{}^{10^{n},1}\;,\;X_{[1]}{}^{10^{n},9}\;,\;C^{10^{n},9,2}\;,\;\dots

(5.148)

where the primary zero-form
C_{10^{n},9,2}
is the first in the tower

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}(h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1})

\displaystyle=\{C^{(m)}_{10^{n},9,2,1^{m}}\,|\;m\in\mathbb{N}\,\}=\{C^{(0)}_{10^{n},9,2},C^{(1)}_{10^{n},9,2,1},C^{(2)}_{10^{n},9,2,1,1},\dots\}\;.

(5.149)

In order not to repeat the details of the three-form and six-form sectors, we simply state that the primary zero-form
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},9,2}
can be expressed as the curvature tensor

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a^{1}[10],\dots,a^{n}[10],b[9],c[2]}=\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}h^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}\;,

(5.150)

for the higher dual graviton
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
.
This can be shown either by solving the unfolded equations for
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},9,2}
in terms of
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
or by using the generalised Poincaré lemma.
It is immediate to see that this curvature is invariant under

\displaystyle\delta h^{(n)}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[8],c}=\left[\begin{matrix}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\lambda^{(1)}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n-1}[9],|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{9}],b[8],c}+\partial_{[b_{1}|}\lambda^{(2)}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],|b_{2}\dots b_{8}],c}\\
\hbox{}+\partial_{c}\,\lambda^{(3)}_{a^{1}[9],\dots,a^{n}[9],b[8]}\end{matrix}\right]_{9^{n},8,1}\;,

(5.151)

where
[\,\dots]_{9^{n},8,1}
denotes a projection onto the GL
(11)
irreducible
\mathbb{Y}[9^{n},8,1]
tableau.

Duality relation between
h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1}
and
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
.

As before, we will first propose the duality relation between
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
and
h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1}
since it has a different form to the duality relations at higher levels.
This duality relation takes the form

\displaystyle\omega^{(2)}{}_{a_{1}\dots a_{9}|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}\;\propto\;\varepsilon_{b_{1}\dots b_{10}}{}^{p}\,\omega^{(1)}{}_{c_{1}\dots c_{8}|pa_{1}\dots a_{9},d}\;.

(5.152)

Similar to what we found in the three-form and six-form sectors, this relation is gauge-covariant when the parameters obey the constraint

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{8}]|b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}-\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{7}||b_{1}\dots b_{10},|a_{8}a_{9}]c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}\propto\delta_{a_{1}\dots a_{9}p,b_{1}\dots b_{10}}\partial_{d}\alpha_{c_{1}\dots c_{8}}{}^{p}\;,

(5.153)

where
\delta_{p[n],q[n]}
denotes
\delta_{p[n]}^{q[n]}
with all the indices lowered.
We have used the previous constraint (
5.30
) to obtain the new constraint (
5.153
), which does not tell us that
\omega^{(2)}{}_{[9]}{}^{10,8,1}
needs to be gauge-invariant, but only that its gauge transformation is related to the dual gravity nine-form parameter
\alpha^{9}
that we introduced in Section
3.3
.

Taking derivatives of the duality relation (
5.152
) leads to a gauge-invariant relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[d_{1}}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(2)}{}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}\dots c_{9}],d_{2}]}\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{e}\partial_{e}\partial_{[d_{1}}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}h^{(1)}{}_{b_{2}\dots b_{10}],c_{2}\dots c_{9}],d_{2}]}\;,

(5.154)

and taking appropriate traces leads to the equations of motion for each field.
In terms of the curvature tensor
C^{(0)}_{10,10,9,2}
, the equations of motion for
h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1}
that follow from (
5.152
) are

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathrm{Tr}_{1,2})^{10}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,&\quad\mathrm{Tr}_{3,4}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,3})^{9}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,&\quad(\mathrm{Tr}_{1,4})^{2}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,\\
(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,3})^{9}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,&\quad(\mathrm{Tr}_{2,4})^{2}(C_{10,10,9,2})&=0\;,\end{aligned}\qquad 1\leq i<j\leq n\;.

(5.155)

Equation (
5.154
) can be expressed in terms of
C^{(0)}_{10,10,9,2}\in\mathscr{T}(h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1})
and
C^{(1)}_{10,9,2,1}\in\mathscr{T}(h^{(1)}_{9,8,1})
:

\displaystyle C^{(0)}{}_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{9},d_{1}d_{2}}=\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{e}C^{(1)}{}_{b_{1}\dots b_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{9},d_{1}d_{2},e}\;.

(5.156)

Integrating this three times leads to the first-order relation

\displaystyle\partial_{[a_{1}}h^{(2)}{}_{a_{2}\dots a_{10}],b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}+\partial_{[b_{1}}\Xi_{b_{2}\dots b_{9}]|a_{1}\dots a_{10},c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}+\partial_{d}\Upsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10},b_{1}\dots b_{9},c_{1}\dots c_{8}}\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a_{1}\dots a_{10}}{}^{e}\left(10\,\partial_{[e}h^{(1)}{}_{b_{1}\dots b_{9}],c_{1}\dots c_{8},d}+\partial_{[c_{1}}\Theta_{c_{2}\dots c_{8}]|eb_{1}\dots b_{9},d}\right)\;.

(5.157)

The arbitrary tensors
\Xi_{8|10,8,1}
and
\Theta_{7|10,1}
have the same structure as
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,8,1}
in (
5.147a
) and
\alpha_{[7]}{}^{10,1}
in (
5.27a
), respectively, so their components are interpreted either as the extra fields that are associated with
h^{(2)}_{9,9,8,1}
and
h^{(1)}_{9,8,1}
or as the components of
\alpha_{[8]}{}^{10,8,1}
and
\alpha_{[7]}{}^{10,1}
that can be shifted away with gauge-for-gauge symmetries.
The field
\Upsilon_{10,9,8}
does not seem to originate from the unfolded equations or gauge symmetries.

Duality relations for higher dual gravitons.

We now propose first-order on-shell duality relations between
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
and
h^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},8,1}
that take the form

\displaystyle\omega^{(n)}{}_{a[9]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[8],f}\;\propto\;\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p}\omega^{(n-1)}{}_{a[9]|pc[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[8],f}\;.

(5.158)

The gauge parameter constraints for these higher duality relations are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{[a_{1}}\alpha_{a_{2}\dots a_{9}]|b[10],c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[8],f}-\beta_{[a_{1}\dots a_{8}||b[10],|a_{9}]c[9],d^{1}[9],\dots,d^{n-3}[9],e[8],f}\hskip 56.9055pt\\
\propto\varepsilon_{b[10]}{}^{p_{1}}\varepsilon_{p_{1}c[9]}{}^{p_{2}}\varepsilon_{p_{2}d^{1}[9]}{}^{p_{3}}\cdots\varepsilon_{p_{n-2}d^{n-3}[9]}{}^{p_{n-1}}\varepsilon_{p_{n-1}a[9]}{}^{p_{n}}\partial_{f}\alpha_{e[8]p_{n}}\;.\end{split}

(5.159)

In contrast to the three-form and six-form sectors of the theory where gauge-invariance of the duality relations forces the gauge-invariance of all first-order connections
\omega
for the higher dual fields, here we find that the gauge parameter constraints do not force the first-order connections in the gravity sector to be gauge-invariant, but instead their gauge variations are all related to the dual gravity parameter
\alpha^{9}
in Section
3.3
.

Taking derivatives leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{1}_{1}}h^{(n)}{}_{a^{1}_{2}\dots a^{1}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}\hskip 142.26378pt

\displaystyle\propto\varepsilon_{a^{1}[10]}{}^{d}\partial_{d}\partial_{[c_{1}}\partial_{[b_{1}}\partial_{[a^{n}_{1}|}\dots\partial_{[a^{2}_{1}}h^{(n-1)}{}_{a^{2}_{2}\dots a^{2}_{10}],\dots,|a^{n}_{2}\dots a^{n}_{10}],b_{2}\dots b_{9}],c_{2}]}\;,

(5.160)

and taking traces leads to the equations of motion of each field.
Expressing
C^{(0)}_{10^{n},9,2}\in\mathscr{T}(h^{(n)}_{9^{,}8,1})
and
C^{(1)}_{10^{n-1},9,2,1}\in\mathscr{T}(h^{(n-1)}_{9^{n-1},8,1})
as curvatures, the equations of motion for
h^{(n)}_{9^{n},8,1}
can be written in the compact form

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathrm{Tr}_{i,j})^{10}(C_{10^{n},9,2})&=0\;,&\quad(\mathrm{Tr}_{i,n+1})^{9}(C_{10^{n},9,2})&=0\;,\\
